### ME-50B — synth and expression choices

Checked2026-10-18 against [original manual pp12–13,18–19][M50B-M]. Text inspection; no software/manual downloads or new visual control-count verification.

| Field | Finding / qualification |
|---|---|
| Synth / waveform and controls | **SAW LEAD,MILD FILTER,DEEP FILTER use saw-wave synth sounds; SQUARE uses square-wave**. Allfour require single-note playing. DRIVE/SENS controls filtering; TONE/CUTOFF controls cutoff; D.LEVEL/RESONANCE controls resonance insynthmode,not dryblend. Do not transfer thedrive-mode direct-level meaning to synthmode. |
| Expression / six effect choices | **WAH,RESONANCE,+1 OCTAVE,−1 OCTAVE,SOUND HOLD,KICK DRUM**, alternatives tovolume mode. ±octave andSoundHold require single notes. RESONANCE cancombine withSAW LEAD/SQUARE; onepedaleffect isnot sixsimultaneousblocks. |
| Looper / Sound Hold distinction | SoundHold sustains aplayednote andpedalposition adjustsitslevel; raisingthetoe farenough cancelsit. It is distinctfrom2.8s HOLD DLY recording/layering,not anadditional general-purpose looper. |


| Field | Finding / qualification | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Power / polarity evidence | Roland explicitly approves PSA 200/500mA adapters for ME-50B; its PSA-S specifications state **regulated 9V DC, center-negative, 500mA capacity**. Therefore center-negative compatibility is supported by the named approved supply, rather than sibling-model inference. Device draw remains140mA. PSA-S page lists100–240V50/60Hz input and warns regional versions/plugs differ; verify actual adapter label. | [ME-50B compatibility][M50B-PS]; [PSA-S specifications][M50B-PSAS] |


### ME-50B — startup and unsaved-edit limits

Checked2026-10-18 against the [exact original manual][M50B-M],printed pp6–8,22–23.

| Field | Operating qualification |
|---|---|
| Power / battery fallback | With batteries installed while using the adapter,the manual says operation continues if adapter power is interrupted. This is a fallback claim,not rechargeable-battery charging or a measured switchover-time guarantee. |
| Live / startup state | Power-up turns FILTER/TONE,DRIVE/SYNTH andDELAY/MOD off;expression starts asvolume. HOLD DLY instead entersrecord standby when selected. First entry intoMemory after power-up selects **bank1/patch1**. These states do not imply automatic restoration of the last live setup. |
| Memory / tuner discards temporary edits | P23 explicitly says temporary **tone edits andtempo settings are lost on patch change orentry toTuner**,unless written. Bank selection alone flashes the pending bank;the sound changes after selecting a number pedal. This is separate from the tuner-reference pitch saving behavior above. |


### ME-50B — compressor and FILTER/TONE inventory

Checked2026-10-18 against [original manual pp9–11][M50B-M].

| Field | Category choices / control limits |
|---|---|
| Processing / compressor choices | Five active choices: **NATURAL,LIMITER,160D SOFT,160D HARD,D-COMP**,plusOff. The shared depth knob controls **threshold for the first four,sustain forD-COMP**. The two160D settings represent lower/higher compression ratios;manual gives no numerical ratios here. These are alternatives,not five simultaneous dynamics blocks. |
| Processing / FILTER-TONE coverage | Eleven alternatives comprise three envelope-wah variants,two octave directions,volume swell,fretless simulation,enhancement,low boost,mid scoop andacoustic-electric bass body resonance. Onlyone is selected at atime. Wah variants differ in sweep direction/strength;the octave choices retain the previously documented single-note restriction. |
| Synth / fretless distinction | DEFRETTER explicitly simulates fretless-bass character from a conventional bass;HOLLOW instead adds acoustic-electric body resonance. Neither establishes general guitar-to-acoustic simulation,a sample library oruser IR loading. |


### ME-50B — tapped delay, saved level and recording standby

Checked 2026-10-18 against [original manual printed pp.7, 16–17][M50B-M]. Text inspection only; no unit operation.

| Field | Finding / qualification |
|---|---|
| Delay / tap versus saved range | ANALOG DLY, 0–400 ms DLY, 300–1400 ms DLY and CHO+DELAY support tapped intervals **62–1400 ms**. On Write, delay time is clamped to the selected type’s knob range: 600 ms in 0–400 mode saves as400; 200 ms in 300–1400 saves as300. This is distinct from dotted-eighth tap delay. |
| Effects / modulation tempo | PHASER, FLANGER and CHORUS support tapped cycles **62–2000 ms**. Moving TIME/RATE after tapping restores the knob-defined interval/rate. These are cycle periods, not delay lengths or a BPM specification. |
| Memory / master level | **MASTER LEVEL is saved per patch**. With all effects off, its midpoint provides matching input/output levels; excessive level can distort. Do not treat this knob as a necessarily global volume independent of patch recall. |
| Looper / preparation and quantization | HOLD DLY requires **2.8 seconds** to enter recording standby after selection or restart. With Kick Drum running, tap/record length is quantized and the kick is recorded into HOLD DLY; pedal movement cannot subsequently change that recorded kick level. |

<!-- comparison evidence ends -->

Seller/item, condition, variant, region, bundle, warranty, voucher and current availability remain unknown. This is a search limitation, not proof of market absence.

## ME-90

Consolidated and refreshed **2026-10-16**; partial verification. This is the guitar ME-90, not ME-90B. Original dates, sources and price qualifications remain below. Historical next-model notes are provenance; use research-state.json for current work.

<!-- BOSS-ME-90 source begins -->

Checked **2026-10-15**. Updates an existing [BOSS comparison](BOSS.md)<!-- historical-link:boss-026 --> record; not another model.

### Display and controls

The [manufacturer specification](https://www.boss.info/global/products/me-90/) explicitly identifies a **two-character, seven-segment LED display**, not a graphical LCD. Graphical-screen diagonal/color classification is **N/A**; physical readout dimensions/color are not published in the checked specification. **No touchscreen interface** is documented. Multicolored footswitch status LEDs must not be described as a color graphical display.

The control list rechecks **25 knobs,8 main footswitches,4 buttons**, plus expression toe, rear power and output-mode switches separately. The page confirms36 factory+36 user presets,97 effect types,11 onboard amps,3 user IR slots and a38-second mono looper. Windows/macOS Tone Studio and IR loader remain separate applications; iOS/Android editing needs optional BT-DUAL. This is a scoped review, not a complete current algorithm-list transcription.

Official product-image attempts failed; the segmented-display classification comes from the explicit specification, not an uninspected photo. Original USD observations remain historical. **Next existing-model review: GX-100.**

### ME-90 — original USD and technical provenance

Research date **2026-10-14**. Technical sources are the linked manufacturer's specifications, including current firmware-dependent figures. **Partial records**: `?` means unverified. Dimensions are **W × D × H in mm**; `/max` gives the maximum height with the pedal raised. `K/F/B` = rotary controls / footswitches / separate buttons, excluding the expression toe switch and rear power/mode switches. Price columns are **USD US-market listed/asking observations**, not statistically estimated fair values; see sources below.

Model totals have different inclusion rules. ME-90's selectable alternatives are not all simultaneously present on its panel. A missing NAM claim is not converted into a definitive “No” without model/firmware documentation.

Price retrieval date: **2026-10-14**. Indexed older listings are explicitly less reliable than current, purchasable offers. No guarantee of stock, local Indonesian pricing, delivery cost or completed-sale value.

### ME-90 — creative algorithms and note handling

Checked **2026-11-03** in [manufacturer Parameter Guide,01,copyright2023,pp2–4,6–8,14–16,18](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/ME-90_parameter_eng01_W.pdf). Firmware introduction/current installed version unstated.

**POLY OCTAVE** explicitly accepts polyphonic input. **HARMONIST** andPEDAL FX±1/±2octave bends require single notes. **PITCH SHIFT** spans±2octaves;**TUNE DOWN**−12…0semitones;**S-BEND**−3…+4octaves. Their remaining note-handling/latency isunverified.

**AC SIM** converts electric-to-acoustic;**DEFRETTER** simulates fretless guitar;Single>Hum/Hum>Single changepickup character. **OVERTONE** adds harmonics,**HUMANIZER/VOICE** voice-like tones,**RING MOD** combines oscillator andguitar. These do not establish dedicated tracking synthesis. **SLOW GEAR,FEEDBACKER,FREEZE** provide swell,feedback andsustain.

COMP/FX1 choices shareone section;ToneStudio replaces itsS-BEND position withPOLY OCTAVE,RING MOD orother alternatives perpatch. MOD housesPITCH SHIFT/HARMONIST/OVERTONE;EQ/FX2 canreplaceEQ withthese. PEDAL FXFREEZE replaces itsother modes. Catalogueavailability isnot simultaneousavailability. PresetnameSYNTHY LEAD doesnot prove a synth engine.

ExactShopee search resurfaced existingElevenMusic12356205/25352150117Rp7,450,000,last-monthcache;no freshcondition/stock certification. Earlieroffers retained. No localdownload ordeviceoperation.

The linked connection-guide page exposes a download agreement without a PDF in retrieved text; detailed wireless setup and firmware requirements remain unchecked. | [Reference manual][ME90B-M] p15; [EV-1-WL connection-guide landing page](https://www.boss.info/global/support/by_product/me-90b/owners_manuals/77f6bba2-2c2d-4a68-87d9-582acd9c311d/) |

| Routing / Bluetooth | Computer playback reaches main outputs, phones and balanced XLR; optional Bluetooth audio reaches main outputs/phones but **not balanced XLR**. USB loopback can return computer audio to USB OUT. | [Reference manual][ME90B-M] pp12,14 |

Offline liveset editing is documented, but saving edited memory results requires the unit connected. | [Tone Studio guide][ME90B-B] p1 |

The inspected guide does not publish accepted file formats, sample rates, bit depths or sample/time limits; those remain unknown. | [Tone Studio guide][ME90B-B] p12 |
| Memory / computer library | A liveset holds at most36 memories; Tone Studio holds up to50 livesets, with further sets exported to computer files. Liveset file extension **.tsl**. Export to the unit does not restore memories beyond36. These computer-library limits do not expand the pedal’s36user+36preset onboard memories. | [Tone Studio guide][ME90B-B] pp6–8 |

Checked 2026-10-18 against manufacturer support articles updated 2026-05-01 and a separately identified Shopee hardware offer.

| Power / voltage and adapter capacity | BOSS explicitly states **195 mA at 9 V DC**, compatible with older 200 mA PSA and later 500 mA PSA-S/PSA-S2 adapters. Four alkaline AA provide approximately six hours; batteries can remain installed as backup if the adapter disconnects. This article does not resolve USB power or directly specify socket polarity. | [Official ME-90B power article](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/24403972628251-ME-90B-What-kind-of-power-supplies-does-it-support) |
| Routing / output-select scope | OUTPUT SELECT applies only to quarter-inch OUTPUT jacks. Balanced XLR is fixed to LINE regardless of AMP/LINE switch or OUTPUT SELECT. | [Official output clarification](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/24404249160219-ME-90B-Which-outputs-does-the-OUTPUT-SELECT-setting-apply-to) |
| Looper / phrase storage | Phrases **cannot be saved inside ME-90B**. Manufacturer suggests recording via analog OUTPUT or USB audio, with the dedicated driver for computer recording. This is external audio recording, not onboard loop-file storage. | [Official phrase-storage answer](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/24403936681499-ME-90B-Can-I-save-a-phrase-created-with-the-Phrase-Loop-function-in-the-unit) |
| Memory / cross-model compatibility | Patch data is **not compatible with other ME-series units**. Shared product-family naming does not establish patch interchange. | [Official patch compatibility](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/24403944639643-ME-90B-Is-the-patch-data-compatible-between-ME-90B-and-other-ME-series) |

### ME-90B — live memory and level behavior

Checked 2026-10-18, original reference manual revision01, printed pp2, 6–7, 16–17.

| Field | Finding / qualification | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| I/O / output-volume scope | OUTPUT LEVEL controls main OUTPUT and PHONES volume, **not balanced XLR volume**. This is separate from the AMP/LINE and OUTPUT SELECT restrictions. | [ME90B-M] p2 |
| Memory / manual-mode transition | **N0** applies physical knob settings when entering manual mode; default **N1** retains memory-mode sound settings while allowing individual effects to be toggled. Manual settings are retained when returning to memory mode. | [ME90B-M] p6 |
| Memory / save behavior | Memory write saves sound settings **except OUTPUT LEVEL**. Unsaved edits are lost on power-off or a different memory selection. Writing can start in manual or memory mode; completion enters memory mode. | [ME90B-M] p7 |
| Routing / manual foot control | The manual-mode BLEND pedal can instead toggle SEND/RETURN using system option **r1**; default **r0** toggles BLEND. | [ME90B-M] p16 |
| Expression / reset exception | Factory reset excludes expression-pedal calibration while restoring user memories and other settings. | [ME90B-M] p17 |

These are nested choices within the31 FX or12 MOD top-level selections, not extra simultaneous blocks. Their sum alone does not explain the advertised112. [MS3-G] |

### Effect-total arithmetic reconciliation

| Field | Finding / qualification |
|---|---|
| Processing / count accounting | Guide p14 SUB DELAY has **2 types,Mono/Pan**,1–1000ms; p15 Chorus has **3 output modes**, retained separately from effect-type counting. [MS3-G] |
| Processing / derived total | **Arithmetic inference**, not an explicit manufacturer counting formula: FX31 top-level choices, replacing7 grouped selectors by8 compressor+3 limiter+6 wah+6 bass wah+21 OD/DS+6 bass OD/DS+2 subdelay choices, gives **76**. MOD12, replacing Phaser/Pan by4/2, gives **16**. **76+16+11 DLY+8 REV+1 NS=112**. Shared FX1/FX2 and MOD1/MOD2 libraries are counted once; no duplicate count for block instances. Output modes and continuous parameters excluded. This matches the advertised total under that convention; it does not establish112 simultaneous effects or independent algorithms. [MS3-G], [MS3-P] |

### MS-3 MIDI and performance-control limits

Checked 2026-10-18 using original owner/parameter-guide text only; no files downloaded or device operation.

| Field | Finding / qualification | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| I/O / patch MIDI | Guide p39 lists PATCH MIDI 1–4, but its manual-transmit sentence says 1–8. Retain the internal conflict; eight slots are not established. Each listed setup has channel OFF/1–16, PC OFF/1–128 and two CC number/value pairs. Bank select cannot transmit alone, requires PC, and cannot enable only LSB. | [Parameter guide p39][MS3-G] |
| Synth / Sound Hold limitation | Sound Hold sustains a captured note while another melody is played; simultaneous multiple-note input is unsupported. Assign F1:SOL/TRG or F2:SOL/TRG to a controller. This is not evidence of phrase-loop recording, overdubbing or a synth engine. | [Parameter guide p6][MS3-G] |
| Controls / tuner outputs | Tuner reference 435–445 Hz. MUTE silences output; BYPASS disables effects; THRU retains the current effect sound while tuning. These are different output modes. | [Parameter guide p43][MS3-G] |
| Controls / lock and page buttons | Hold ON/OFF at least two seconds in the play screen to lock buttons and knobs; repeat to unlock. EDIT and ENTER double as page buttons, so page labels do not add two physical buttons to the existing five-button inventory. Hold MEMORY/MANUAL at least two seconds to enter MUTE & TUNER. | [Owner manual p5][MS3-M] |

This corroborates the parameter-guide heading. Its conflicting 1–8 sentence remains preserved above, rather than establishing eight setups. | Controls / Tempo Hold | Set BPM preference to PATCH and TEMPO HOLD ON in the **destination patch**, then Write. The arriving patch retains the previous live BPM. This is tempo persistence, not audio-tail continuation. | [Original application guide p14](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/MS-3_Application_Guide_eng01_W.pdf) |

### MS-3 — eng03 pedal hold and noise-detector scope

Checked **2026-11-04**, [manufacturer Parameter Guide eng03, ©2017, printedpp25–26,40–41](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/MS-3_Parameter_Guide_eng03_W.pdf),49-page original PDF with readable text. Eng03 identifies the document revision, not the installed or latest firmware.

**EXP1 HLD / EXP2 HLD=ON** carries controller-operation state across patch changes **when FUNCTION matches**. The guide explicitly illustrates FOOT VOLUME in both patches: pedal position at the switch continues controlling the arriving patch. OFF does not apply that state. This resolves conditional documented recall; it does not establish global FV endpoints, chain placement, immunity to other assignments or measured glitch-free recall. PREF SYSTEM sharing remains a separate setting.

**NS DETECT** selects INPUT, NS INPUT or FV OUT. The guide recommends FV OUT when using Foot Volume like a guitar volume control, and NS INPUT for its illustrated delay-before-NS arrangement to avoid cutting the spatial effect. Detector selection changes the control path, not the FV audio position or universal tail muting. Display CONTRAST is1–16; the existing FV curve-table/diagram ambiguity remains in eng03. No screenshot, download, pedal test or setting change.

### SE-50 — input controls and patch-selection behavior

Checked2026-10-18 against [French manufacturer manual](https://fr.scribd.com/document/644989539/Boss-SE-50-fr),©1990,front-panel,input-level andprogram-selection sections. Text-derived;no visual control audit.

| Live / patch stepping | One **FS-5U atNUMBER SHIFT** advances oneprogram perpress;holding does not auto-repeat. Two switches through **PCS-31** provide increase/decrease. A configurable range limits stepping;reversing its endpoints gives descending order. |
| Memory / power-on recall andediting | Power-on recalls the previously used program. Parameter edits are temporary untilwritten;changing programs orpowering off loses unsaved changes. This is distinct from automatic recall of an already stored program. |

### SE-50 — nominal sensitivity and global effects state

Checked2026-10-18 against the [French BOSS manual][SE50-FR], input-level and effects-on/off sections. Manufacturer-authored mirror text; no device or visual test.

| Required field | Evidence / qualification |
|---|---|
| I/O / nominal input sensitivity | Rear LEVEL selects **−20dBm or+4dBm**. If overload remains frequent at−20dBm even with the input controls at minimum, the manual directs selecting+4dBm. These are nominal sensitivity settings, **not maximum input ratings**; the separately documented overload warning remains6dB below clipping. |
| Live / global effects state | Programs can be selected while effects are off; the global on/off state is retained through other operations. This does not establish stored per-program bypass state, effect trails or power-cycle retention. |

### SE-50 — stereo pitch and gated flanger behavior

Checked2026-10-18 against the original©1990 [French BOSS manual][SE50-FR],Stereo Pitch Shifter and Stereo Flanger sections,printed pp48–51. OCR text only;no audio or visual test.

| Processing / Stereo Pitch Shifter | Per-channel **−12 to+12 semitones**,fine tuning,and **0–600ms predelay**;feedback and cross-feedback,independent wet/dry levels. **STEREO LINK** synchronizes channels and is required for MIDI fine-pitch control to affect both. Zero predelay can still have processing delay. These are this algorithm’s limits;polyphonic tracking and zero-latency operation are not established. |

| Live / flanger MIDI gate | **GATE ON/OFF(TRIG)** toggles from a momentary MIDI control;**GATE ON/OFF** changes state while held. Other destinations include flanger rate,gate rate and master level. These are algorithm-specific assignments,not direct analog pedal sockets. |

### SE-70 — single-note synthesis and external-carrier vocoding

[A] explicitly requires **single notes**, muting each preceding note, with direct instrument connection to **L(MONO)**. Guitar Synth targets electric guitar; Bass Synth specifies **four-string electric bass**. Chords, harmonics and accidental adjacent strings can cause errors. **Square/Saw**, octave shifts **+1/0/−1/−2**, and semitone-snapping **Chromatic** are documented; snapping suppresses sub-semitone bends/vibrato. These are tracking-synth controls, not input-note polyphony or an extra instrument engine. Exact pitch-shifter range/polyphony and acoustic-conversion algorithms remain unverified.

[V] describes an external source at the left input and voice/other modulator at the right. **Vocoder1:12bands plus distortion/chorus/delay/reverb;Vocoder2:21bands plus reverb.** This external-carrier processing is separate from Guitar/Bass Synth and does not establish onboard polyphonic synthesis or direct mic-level/phantom support. The article was available as indexed manufacturer text; direct opening returned404. **Later original-guide check below finds a10-versus12-band conflict andadditional Vocoder2 chorus; retain this paragraph as the support article’s claim.**

Next: complete the original algorithm-guide census,physical control/connector verification and exact Indonesian offers before considering active admission. Retain independent footswitch-display/expression/GT-Pro priorities. No document/software downloaded or device operated.

### SE-70 — pitch modes, short sampling and third-party editor

Checked **2026-11-04**. [Original manufacturer algorithm guide](https://cdn.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SE-70_PA.pdf), indexed **Pitch Shifter,Key Changer,Repeat Play** sections. Full-text opening exposes no lines; screenshot calls supplied no inspectable image. The mirror’s contents listing is a retrieval lead, not a verified complete census. Firmware applicability remains unstated.

| Field | Source-backed distinction |
|---|---|
| Pitch / range and response | **±2octaves**. Higher numbered modes respond more slowly with fewer fluctuations; Inverse1/2 offer different reverse response lengths. No measured latency or input-polyphony claim. |
| Pitch / stereo linking | Stereo Pitch Shift’s **Stereo Link On** applies left settings to both channels and ignores right settings while retaining stereo placement. Stereo channels and generated voices do not prove chord handling. |
| Special / Repeat Play | **2,000ms sampling**, automatic repetitive playback after recording, reverse options and playback intervals up to20seconds. The interval is not20seconds of sample capacity or a modern multitrack looper specification. |
| Special / Vocal Canceler and Key Changer | Center-field attenuation can affect other centered sounds and fail with reverberant/off-center vocals. Key Changer uses semitone/fine adjustments; its modes trade response speed for fewer fluctuations. Dedicated acoustic conversion remains unverified. |

**Third-party editor, not BOSS software:** [X-70 developer release page](https://monophreak.com/boss-se-70-ctrlr-vst-au-plugin/) labels the current release **v15.1**, advertises **Windows Intel,Mac Intel andMac Silicon** builds,and reports a Stereo Phaser transfer fix. Minimum OS versions andnative-versus-Rosetta compatibility are not independently established. Its standalone editor is described as available; DAW integration remains qualified by the developer’s Logic parameter-initialization issue and pipeline wording. The older [developer guide,2024-06-03/2025-01-30](https://monophreak.com/x-70-user-guide/) describes component/VST use; that historical guide alone doesnot establish a current production plugin release.

The guide specifies **two-way MIDI**,algorithm-aware parameter loading andReload/Send operations. Experimental Live mode transmits broader parameter changes than the device’s assigned CC controls; the author leaves memory-wear implications unresolved. This documents developer-supported editing,not USB audio,global analog volume orsoftware compatibility tested here. No software,document ordependency downloaded locally; no MIDI sent.

SE-70 remains a partial lead outsideactivecounts. Full original algorithm census,physical controls/connector sizes andqualified used/new IDR remain pending;independent LCD-first,expression andGT-Pro/general queues remain intact.

### SE-70 — panel-text control count and shared expression socket

Indexed [original owner manual,p9](https://cdn.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SE-70_OM.pdf) enumerates independently adjustable Input Level L/R and CONTROL: **3 rotary controls**,with the CONTROL push counted once. EXIT,WRITE,PARAMETER←/→,CONTROL1,UTILITY give **6 front buttons**;power separate. No onboard footswitch is identified in this complete front-panel inventory. These are text-inventory counts,not a new image inspection;the earlier wholly unknown count is narrowed accordingly.

[Owner manual,p29](https://cdn.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SE-70_OM.pdf): the shared socket selects **Effect Remote** for FS-5L whole-unit effects on/off or **Exp Pedal** for EV-5/FV-300L parameter control. CONTROL2/3 accepts FS-5U/FS-5L using PCS-31,white→Control2/red→Control3. This doesnot establish passive-pedal pinout,resistance orglobal volume across patches.

Partial lead remains outsideactivecounts. Original named-algorithm census is now documented;full parameter/routing checks,connector diameters,visual corroboration,qualified IDR andall-preset volume remain open. No local download ordevice operation.

### SE-70 — patch assignments, effects-off behavior and parallel routing

Checked **2026-11-04** against indexed [original owner manual, pp18,32–33](https://cdn.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SE-70_OM.pdf), and [original algorithm guide, Algorithms Geared for Use With a Mixer](https://cdn.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SE-70_PA.pdf). Firmware applicability remains unstated; no hardware test.

| Field | Documented scope |
|---|---|
| Expression / assignment | **Four assignments per patch**; targets depend on the algorithm. Sources include Exp Pedal, Control1–3 and MIDI. The common targets listed for all algorithms are Tuner, Metronome, Metronome Level and Effect On/Off. This is the documented **PER PATCH** path; a global volume override remains **UNVERIFIED**. |
| Expression / range and recall | Min/Max are target-specific and may be reversed. The illustrated Master Level range is0–100,not a certified mute/dB specification. Live controller changes are temporary and revert on patch change; held-position recall is unverified. |
| Bypass / effects-off | **Direct** outputs dry sound; **Mute** silences effects-off output. The manual recommends Mute for mixer send/return use to avoid adding dry signal. This switch behavior doesnot establish continuous pedal attenuation or tail/recorded-sample coverage. |
| Routing / algorithms140–145 | **Mono+Mono** keeps the two channel effects as independent mono outputs; **Stereo Mix** preserves each effect in stereo and mixes them at output. These are six fixed mixer-oriented algorithms,not arbitrary duplicate blocks or two independently stereo output pairs. |

The earlier global-expression uncertainty is narrowed to a documented per-patch assignment scheme,not converted into proof that a global override is absent. Dedicated headphones are already listed; their pedal/bypass coverage remains unverified. The front CONTROL encoder selects patches by hand; no onboard preset down/up footswitch is identified. **218W×240Dmm** exceeds the preferred200×140 footprint. SE-70 remains a partial lead outside active counts.

Next: verify parameter-level volume placement andminimum/maximum behavior,connector diameters,visual corroboration andqualified IDR;preserve LCD-first andindependent research queues. No local download, MIDI transmission ordevice operation.

Manufacturer zero-latency language is not a measured result. Dedicated acoustic simulation remains unverified. |
| Processing / memory | Same page: three synth sections plus four FX; 99 user and 70 preset patches. Installed firmware and patch contents remain unverified; published support version is qualified below. |
| Hardware / display / footprint | Same page: 132×64 backlit graphic LCD; 255W×191D×70H mm, 1.75kg excluding adapter; 400mA draw. Exceeds preferred 200W×140D. Screen diagonal/color/touch and USB/battery operation remain unverified; named-supply voltage/polarity resolved in the follow-up below. No separate footswitch displays established. |
| I/O / software | Same page: INPUT, THRU, RETURN; MAIN R/MONO and L/PHONES; SUB R/MONO and L; MIDI IN and OUT/THRU; USB-B; shared TRS EXP/CTL4,5; DC IN. Tone Studio editor for Windows/macOS; dated Windows application requirements and separate driver scope are qualified below. |
| Controls / headphones / navigation | [Own English manual e01, ©2015, pp2–4,9](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-300_e01_W.pdf): no built-in treadle in panel inventory; four footswitches. CTL2/CTL3 default to patch down/up but can become control switches. Six rotary controls: OUTPUT LEVEL, SELECT and parameters1–4. Seven named separate buttons identified in the text-derived census below; visual completeness unverified. Headphones use MAIN L/PHONES with R/MONO disconnected. USB carries audio/MIDI, not established USB power. |
| Expression / assignment versus recall | [Parameter Guide/Sound List e02, ©2015, pp13,17–20](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-300_ParamGuide_e02.pdf): up to three analog-pedal assignments; **CTL MODE=SYSTEM shares settings across patches**. PATCH COM LEVEL and SUBOUT LEVEL are listed targets; MAIN PATCH LEVEL controls MAIN output. TARGET MIN/MAX and ACT RANGE are separate. EXP PEDAL HOLD recalls pedal state; it does not create assignment. Numeric level endpoints, bypass/USB/phones/tail/loop coverage, actual M-VAVE wiring and toe support remain unverified. |
| Special / pitch qualification | Same guide pp4–5,7,9: oscillator pitch and bend are synthesis parameters; conventional harmony/pitch-shifter FX remain unverified. LO-FI, SLICER, SLOW GEAR, sequencer and oscillator hold are distinct processes. Hold prevents new synth sounds while active. No measured tracking-quality claim. |

### SY-300 — exact FX inventory and synth-sequencer limits

Checked **2026-11-04** against [Parameter Guide e02,©2015,printedpp4–5](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-300_ParamGuide_e02.pdf) and [owner manual e01,pp3–6](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-300_e01_W.pdf). Composite types remain single choices. The guide explicitly permits the same type in all four FX processors;21 is not a simultaneous-effect count. |
| Synth / sequencing and layer boundary | Up to16 programmed pitch steps can produce melodies/arpeggios from one played note. OSC2/3 can link to OSC1. **INPUT waveform disables sequencer and pitch-range Layer processing**;OSC1 sequencer parameters can remain visible for linking. This is not an audio phrase looper or evidence of16-note input polyphony. |

| Controls / tuner-access condition | Normal patch-down/up pair also enters tuner together, but this shortcut becomes unavailable if either switch is reassigned as CTL. Two Quick pages expose eight parameter assignments,not eight additional knobs orscreens. |

### SY-300 — subtype counts, USB routing and insertion boundary

Subtypes sit within the21 FX families,not extra processors. RE-SYNTH processes recorded dry guitar;DIRECT OFF sends local processing only to computer for DAW-effect monitoring. **Power-off resets routing to STANDARD**. Dry guitar is output in all routes;complete stream/channel mapping remains unverified. |

| Memory / INSERT loss boundary | WRITE,EXCHANGE,INITIALIZE andINSERT are distinct. INSERT shifts user-area contents andloses overflowing **U99**;it does not add capacity. No patch operation performed. |

### SY-300 — waveform processing and current macOS qualification

Checked **2026-11-04**. Partial lead outside 402;  no ownership or membership change.

[Parameter Guide e02, ©2015, pp4–5](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-300_ParamGuide_e02.pdf) documents eight oscillator choices: **SIN, SAW, TRI, SQR, PWM, DETUNE SAW, NOISE, INPUT**. INPUT uses the incoming signal and disables PITCH through RING, as well as sequencer/layer controls noted above; it is not another tracking-synth waveform. SYNC resets OSC2/3 against OSC1; RING multiplies oscillator signals. Neither establishes conventional guitar-audio harmony. Filters offer LPF, HPF, BPF, PKG plus bypass. Two LFOs offer seven shapes (SIN, SAW UP, SAW DOWN, TRI, SQR, RANDOM, S&H), modulating pitch, filter, amplitude and PWM where applicable. These are modulation choices, not extra synth voices or input-note limits. Numeric pitch ranges remain unverified.

[BOSS macOS compatibility matrix](https://www.boss.info/global/support/support_news/25090811m1/), **updated 2026-10-30**, lists SY-300 **Hardware & Driver** and **Exclusive Application** separately: both **Yes(Download)** for **macOS 15 Sequoia and 26 Tahoe**, both **Under Testing** for **macOS 27 Golden Gate**. This supersedes the current-matrix uncertainty above; the older March 2017 Sierra introduction remains historical. It does not establish native Apple-silicon execution or local tested operation. The [1.1.1 application page](https://www.boss.info/global/support/by_product/sy-300/updates_drivers/391a4df2-958b-42f8-b089-146a978d71c1/) links that matrix and requires the appropriate USB driver.

Next: imports/capture and practice-feature evidence, USB stream specifications and expression endpoints/all-route coverage. Earlier completed control/subtype/OS tasks remain historical checkpoints, not the current continuation cursor. No download, installation or device operation.

### SY-300 — liveset imports versus computer practice playback

Checked **2026-11-04**, [Using BOSS TONE STUDIO for SY-300 e03,©2015,pp2,4–8,12–15](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/BTS_SY-300_e03_W.pdf). Partial lead outside402; historical software documentation, not a local test.

- **Patch data:** library backup groups device patches into a liveset; export/import uses **.tsl**. A computer liveset holds up to400 patches, not400 device slots. Restoring/copying patches can overwrite destinations. The documented backup does not establish recovery of system settings, firmware or audio.
- **Practice playback:** Tone Studio’s computer player accepts **44.1kHz,16-bit linear,stereo WAV**. It can repeat one track or the playlist; speed75–125%, pitch−600…+600cents, level0–100. These describe backing-track playback, not USB stream specifications, guitar pitch algorithms or an onboard phrase looper/drum machine.
- **Import scope:** livesets are patch settings; WAV import feeds the computer player. Neither documents oscillator-sample loading, cabinet IRs, native/converted NAM or proprietary capture. Those capabilities remain unverified.
- **Connection:** offline liveset organization is documented, but saving edited patch results requires the device connection. SY-300 DAW CTRL is a Windows-only selection, distinct from the editor’s SY-300 device entry.

Next: defer unestablished import/capture and onboard practice support; investigate USB stream formats and expression endpoints/route coverage when exact primary evidence is available. Preserve independent priority queues and all earlier sources.

### SY-300 — expression travel and output-off boundaries

Checked **2026-11-04**, [Parameter Guide e02,©2015,pp11,13,17–18,20](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-300_ParamGuide_e02.pdf). Partial lead outside402.

**ACT RANGE MIN0–126/MAX1–127** restricts pedal travel; normal full travel uses0/127. Outside that window the target stays at its endpoint. Target MIN/MAX are parameter-dependent; reversing their order reverses response. Changing target requires rechecking endpoints. This does not establish numeric volume limits or pedal wiring.

**MAINOUT OFFMOD:** BYPASS removes synth/effects and passes dry guitar; SYNTH OFF retains FX/routing and external-return processing. **SUBOUT OFFMOD:** MUTE,BYPASS,ALWAYS ON; even ALWAYS ON loses synth when MAIN uses SYNTH OFF. Continuous EXP attenuation in these states remains unverified.

SUBOUT SYS CTL overrides patch SUB settings; THRU can feed SUB only with that system control enabled. OUTPUT LEVEL MODE assigns the physical knob to MAIN+SUB,MAIN,SUB or neither; it does not assign EXP. Headphone,USB,tail andrecorded-loop coverage remain unresolved. No hardware operation.

### SY-1000 — shared volume targets, controls and revision boundaries

Checked **2026-11-04**, documentation only. This narrows the initial unknowns without admitting the partial lead to active counts.

- **Shared volume assignment:** [Reference eng01, p68](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng01_W.pdf) lists EXP1/EXP2 targets **FV1, FV2, FV1+TUNER, FV2+TUNER**, plus **PATCH LEVEL100 (0–100)** and **PATCH LEVEL200 (0–200)**. Together with SYSTEM preference in owner p17 and [eng02 p69](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng02_W.pdf), this documents a shared volume assignment without individual target editing in every patch. This is a source-based conclusion, not a hardware test; eng01 target-list equivalence across every later firmware is unverified. [Eng02 p66](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng02_W.pdf) defines PATCH LEVEL as overall patch volume; INST LEVEL and NOR MIX targets are narrower and must not be substituted as whole-output controls.
- **Recall versus assignment:** [Original Japanese reference jpn01, p87](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_jpn01_W.pdf) and [manufacturer-authored English reference on mirror, p87](https://gr300.com/owners/Boss_SY-1000_Reference_Manual.pdf) agree: HOLD Off loads saved patch values; HOLD On retains a controller value **when its assigned parameter matches the preceding patch**. This does not create an assignment. The English mirror also distinguishes hand OUTPUT LEVEL knob destinations MAIN, SUB and MAIN+SUB from pedal control. USB, bypass, delay/reverb tails and loop coverage remain unverified; zero on a parameter scale is not a measured all-route mute.
- **Foot-volume endpoints:** [Manufacturer reference copy indexed at Bax, p46](https://www.bax-shop.nl/downloads/products/9000-0058-1907/boss_sy-1000_reference_manual.pdf) gives FX-category FOOT VOLUME minimum, maximum and pedal position each **0–100**, with SLOW1/SLOW2/NORMAL/FAST curves. This is the FX-category entry; do not automatically transfer its range/globality to every dedicated FV1/FV2 setting. Full mirror file returned unsupported content type; exact revision equivalence remains unverified.
- **Controls and connections:** [Owner eng01, pp3–5,20](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_eng01_W.pdf): **7 rotary /8 footswitches /8 separate panel buttons**, counting six parameter knobs plus OUTPUT LEVEL; INST, EFFECTS, CTL, EXIT, WRITE, SYSTEM and two PAGE buttons. Rear power is separate. Ports: 13-pin GK; quarter-inch GUITAR INPUT, SEND, RETURN, MAIN L/PHONES and R/MONO, SUB L and R/MONO; two TRS CTL/EXP; USB-B; MIDI IN, OUT/THRU; DC IN. Headphones require MAIN L/PHONES with R/MONO disconnected. EV-30, FV-500L/H and EV-5 are named; M-VAVE compatibility/pinout is unverified. No built-in treadle in this panel inventory.

### SY-1000 — creative parameters and algorithm-specific note handling

Checked **2026-11-04** against indexed manufacturer-authored reference pages. The official full files exceed the web reader size limit; the English mirror also exceeds it. These are page-level checks, not a complete manual or installed-firmware audit.

| Algorithm / role | Exact evidence and limits |
|---|---|
| HARMONIST | [Reference eng01 p47](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng01_W.pdf), corroborated by [original reference copy p47](https://gr300.com/owners/Boss_SY-1000_Reference_Manual.pdf): **single notes only**, muting other strings. Offers one or two generated voices; the latter can be mono/stereo output. Harmony spans −2 to +2 octaves or USER scale. Generated voices/channels do not establish chord tracking. |
| PEDAL BEND | [Original reference copy p49](https://gr300.com/owners/Boss_SY-1000_Reference_Manual.pdf): **chords unsupported**; endpoint pitch **−24…+24 semitones**, pedal position0–100. This restriction is independent of OSC Synth poly mode or the number of INST parts. |
| PITCH SHIFTER | [Reference eng01 p50](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng01_W.pdf): −24…+24 semitones, fine±50cents, one/two generated voices. FAST→MEDIUM→SLOW trades slower response for less modulation; **MONO mode explicitly requires single notes**. Other modes’ chord support remains unverified. Indexed table extraction splits PS1/PS2 rows; do not infer that MONO is available only to PS2 from that layout. |
| OSC SYNTH | [Reference eng02 p29](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng02_W.pdf): two oscillators with SINGLE, DUAL, SYNC and RING configurations; **MONO/POLY SW** selects note behavior, with MONO sounding only one note from a chord. This GK-dependent engine remains distinct from normal-input Dynamic Synth. Two oscillators are not an input-note ceiling; normal-input Dynamic Synth chord handling remains unresolved. |
| HUMANIZER / LO-FI | [Reference eng02 p48](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng02_W.pdf): Humanizer switches a/e/i/o/u vowels by picking or automatically; it is vowel processing. Lo-fi offers bit-depth Off/15…1 and sample-rate Off/½…1⁄32 controls, not converter specifications or synth voices. |
| SITAR SIM / SLOW GEAR | [Reference eng02 p52](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/SY-1000_reference_eng02_W.pdf): Sitar Sim models sitar character with resonance/buzz; Slow Gear/Bass shapes the onset into a violin-like swell. The latter does not establish a violin instrument engine. |
| SLICER / SOUND HOLD | Same p52: Slicer has **20 patterns**, rate/tempo and restart trigger; saved patches reset its trigger Off. **Sound Hold does not work properly with simultaneous notes**; its trigger is intended for a footswitch and also saves Off. The stereo label does not remove this single-note limitation. |

These alternatives share FX1–FX3; their presence does not establish unlimited simultaneous combinations. Earlier sources, normal-patch restrictions, separate expression-volume scope and unverified acoustic-conversion/IR/NAM/capture fields remain intact.

### SY-1000 — named power-supply mapping

[Roland/BOSS adapter table, updated **2025-07-15**, PDF pp1,4](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/roland_ac_adaptor_information.pdf?lang=nl-NL) explicitly maps **SY-1000→PSB-1U**. That supply is **9V DC,2A/18W**, with **100–240V AC50/60Hz** input. Supply capacity is separate from the recorded **800mA device draw**. Polarity, connector dimensions, seller-included supply and regional mains cable remain unverified; do not borrow PSA-S polarity or another device’s battery-adapter instructions. No hardware operation or local download performed.

### SY-1000 — normal-input effects access and saved patch type

Checked **2026-11-05**, Roland’s [GUITAR INPUT limitations article,updated October21,2022](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/360039752331-SY-1000-Are-there-limitations-on-the-functionality-if-my-guitar-bass-is-connected-only-the-GUITAR-INPUT-jack). Conventional input uses NORMAL patches;selecting a GK patch leaves its synth INST silent. Only Dynamic Synth is selectable as INST TYPE in NORMAL patches,andRoland explicitly says its sound differs between NORMAL andGK patches. The **EFFECT block has no additional restriction from using GUITAR INPUT**. This does not remove individual algorithm note-handling rules orestablish unlimited concurrency.

The [patch-type procedure,updated July27,2023](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/360040631111-SY-1000-How-To-Change-Patch-Type-To-Normal-Patch-For-Guitar-Input) selects MST→page2→PATCH TYPE,then confirms NORMAL. The GK icon disappears;the change requires **WRITE/SAVE for that patch**. This is not automatic conversion ofevery stored patch orglobal volume assignment. Firmware applicability is unstated;no change was made onhardware.

This narrows ordinary-pickup usability whileleaving Dynamic Synth chord handling andfull current category/concurrency verification unresolved. Existing Shopee cache-price conflict is unchanged;partial lead remains outsideactive counts. No download,device connection orsave operation.

### VE-1 — direct-control exclusion and timed memory cycle

Retain vocal-only membership,outside requested guitar capability table.156W×116Dmm fits;stereo-mini PHONES/LINE documented.

Hold onboard pedal≥2seconds:memory cycles A/B/C/manual at1second intervals;release selects. This is not independent down/up. External MEMORY INC advances only;no decrement function in the seven-option table. Pedal bypass switches echo/pitch correction butleavesEnhance active. Direct-pedal position,endpoints andrecall are inapplicable to documented inventory;pedal headphone/bypass/tail/recorded-loop attenuation remains unverified. ### VE-2 — original control census and guitar-volume exclusion

Checked2026-10-28 against [own English e04 manual][VE2-M],single-sheet PDF,original numbered panel text. **5 rotaries/1 footswitch/4 buttons**:BALANCE,TYPE,KEY,ECHO,rearMIC SENS;VARIATION,AUTO HARMONIST,ENHANCE,MEMORY. Rear POWER/PHANTOM/GND switches are additional. This resolves the text-based control-count gap;not fresh visual inspection.

Onboard memory selection first requires bypass byholding the pedal≥2seconds,then another hold cycles A–C atone-second intervals;release selects the displayed memory. This is **timed selection,not independent preset down/up switches**. MEMORY button also selects memories.

GuitarTHRU is unchanged;guitar audio is absent from XLR,PHONES/LINE andUSB recording. The external VARIATION/MEMORY socket accepts switches,not analog EXP. Thus156W×116Dmm fits,but fails the required processed-guitar/direct-analog path. Vocal membership retained,outside guitar-volume table. Pedal volume position/range/recall/bypass/tails/recorded-loop coverage has no documented analog path. No hardware test ordownload.

MASTER NoiseThres is a separate sound-level noise adjustment,not a documented second dynamics engine. Manual pp16–17. |

### VE-5 — switch controls versus continuous expression

Checked **2026-10-28** against identity-matched [2012 manufacturer manual copy][VE5-COPY],pp9–11,15,20,andindexed [original p15][VE5-PDF]. Complete inventory has no treadle/analog EXP;CTL1/2 is documented forfoot switches,not continuous expression. CTL functions include SOUND Inc/Dec,FAV Inc/Dec,effect/harmony toggles andloop actions. Their system-wide persistence does not establish global volume assignment. No onboard footswitches;hand SOUND/favorites are separate fromexternal navigation.

Microphone input is documented;manual warns against another processor’s output. Normal guitar-pickup suitability remains unclassified;retain vocal-only membership,outside requested guitar capability table. Bare178W×108Dmm fits;withholder178W×159Dmm exceeds depth. Stereo-mini PHONES/LINE confirmed. Direct-pedal position/endpoints/recall are inapplicable to documented inventory;pedal bypass/headphone/tail/recorded-loop attenuation remains unverified. Existing headphone electrical conflict andvisual-control qualifications remain open;no hardware test.

Checked **2026-10-14**. **Partial records**: `?` means unverified, not No. Dimensions are **W×D×H, mm**. K/F/B = rotary controls / main footswitches / separate buttons; rear power, phantom and ground-lift toggles are additional. Used and new prices are separate **USD US-market asking/listing observations**, not sold-price statistics. Tax, shipping and condition matter.

A guitar input used to detect chords does not automatically provide guitar effects. VE-500's instrument THRU sends the **unprocessed guitar signal** to other pedals. VE-8, by contrast, has its own acoustic-guitar processing path.

### VE-8 — direct expression and memory-navigation boundary

Checked2026-10-28 against [original manual][VE8-M],copyright2016,e02 filename,printedpp5–7,13,15,and[exact manufacturer specifications][VE8-S]. The complete connector list provides **MEMORY INC/DEC TRS for footswitches,not analog EXP**. Its compatible FS-5U/FS-6/FS-7 switching is not continuous pedal control. Existing no-built-in-treadle classification retained.

Dedicated stereo-mini headphones have an independent0–99 level,default50. Memory mode supports onboard footswitch memory switching;the text extraction does not reliably identify the physical down/up pairing. Manual/Stomp effect toggles are separate. Knob pickup waits until the physical knob crosses the recalled setting;this is not expression-position recall.

217W×161Dmm fails both preferred limits. Acoustic-guitar processing is established;ordinary electric-pickup suitability remains unclassified. The documented direct analog volume path fails;placement,range,recall,bypass,headphone,tail andrecorded-loop pedal coverage have no documented analog path. No hardware test ordownload.

Dynamics andDelay each have one listed parameter family;Delay1–4000ms. Harmony has two configurable parts (Double,interval harmony,Unison,Off);interval/depth settings are not additional algorithms. Explicit simultaneous ceiling remains unverified. [Manual pp10,18](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/VE-20_OM.pdf) |
| Editor / IR / NAM / capture | No listed digital editor/import connection;IR,native NAM,converted NAM,capture and USB powering unestablished. No guitar processing inferred. DC polarity resolved below through the model’s named PSA supply. |

### VE-20 — direct-control exclusion and sound-selection mode

Checked **2026-10-28** against own [20-page original manual][VE20-M],pp8–9,14,20. Complete controls/connectors list no treadle oranalog EXP input. Combo INPUT is documented for a direct microphone;manual warns against another effects processor’s output because distortion/undesired effects can result. Normal guitar-pickup suitability remains unclassified;retain vocal-only membership,outside requested guitar capability table.173W×158Dmm fails preferred depth;shared quarter-inch stereo PHONES/LINE confirmed.

Hold HARMONY≥2seconds to enter sound-selection mode:ON/OFF selects preceding sound,HARMONY next;hold HARMONY again≥2seconds restores normal pedal functions. Thus onboard down/up is documented in a dedicated mode. Parameter-guide Level controls the selected sound’s volume;not global pedal assignment. Direct-pedal position,endpoints andrecall are inapplicable to documented inventory;pedal bypass/headphone/tail/recorded-loop attenuation remains unknown. Existing current/nominal-level source conflicts remain unresolved. No hardware test orfresh visual inspection.

### VE-22 — output and loop operation,2026-10-17

| Field | Finding / qualification | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| I/O / output modes | **MONO** sums internal stereo to both XLR outputs;**STEREO** preserves L/R;**FX/DRY** sends mono dry+effect mix to L and bypass to R. The left side is not isolated wet-only audio. OUTPUT settings apply globally and save automatically. | [Reference p21][VE22-M] |
| Looper / recording and control | Recorded phrases **cannot be saved** in device memory. PERFORM records harmony/effect/echo;CHECK records before those processors for sound adjustment. Built-in loop control requires Memory mode;Manual mode needs external switches assigned LOOP/LOOP CTL. Double-tap within one second stops;hold clears. | [Reference pp22–23][VE22-M] |
| Expression / memory behavior | **EXP HOLD** globally retains the previous memory’s pedal value when enabled;automatically saved. Unlike that system setting,edited sound parameters require MEMORY WRITE before memory change or power-off. | [Reference pp8,21][VE22-M] |

Checked 2026-10-18 against own specifications and reference manual revision01.

| I/O / input levels | Mic INPUT nominal **−40 dBu adjustable**, maximum **0 dBu**, **4 kΩ**. AUX nominal **−20 dBu**, maximum **0 dBu**, **27 kΩ**. | [VE22-S] |
| I/O / output levels and loading | XLR L/MONO,R nominal **−40 dBu**, output impedance **600 Ω**, recommended load **≥600 Ω**. PHONES/LINE nominal **−10 dBu**, output impedance **47 Ω**, recommended load **≥47 Ω**. The two connector types do not share the same nominal level. | [VE22-S] |
| Processing / converter ratings | **48 kHz**, **24-bit ADC + AF**, **32-bit DAC**, **32-bit floating-point processing**. These ratings do not establish USB stream bit depth. | [VE22-S] |
| I/O / volume-control scope | PHONES/LINE knob adjusts only PHONES/LINE OUT; it does **not** adjust OUTPUT L/R volume. AUX volume is adjusted on the connected playback device. | [VE22-M] pp2–3 |
| Physical controls / panel lock | Long-press EXIT on the play screen locks MEMORY/HARMONY/EFFECT/ECHO knobs and MENU; long-press again unlocks. The documented lock scope does not include PHONES/LINE or MIC SENS. | [VE22-M] p2 |

### VE-22 — global expression volume and input eligibility

Checked2026-10-28 against [own reference manual,p16][VE22-M],English revision01,indexed original table. EXP **FUNCTION=VOLUME**, **PREF=SYSTEM** applies the function globally across memories;MEMORY selects per-memory behavior. **MIN andMAX each0–100**,respectively heel andtoe,are unavailable withFUNCTION=OFF. PREF itself is a system parameter. Follow the documented MEMORY WRITE step for edited settings;no setup was performed. This is assignment-scope evidence,distinct from the previously documented EXP HOLD recall. Position relative to effects,bypass,headphone,tail andrecorded-loop attenuation remain unverified.

The [manufacturer instrument-input answer](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/22184017569563-VE-22-Can-I-connect-an-instrument-such-as-a-guitar-or-keyboard-to-apply-effects),updated2026-06-19,limits INPUT to microphones andstates AUX receives no effects. Thus global vocal-volume support does not qualify the requested direct normal-guitar path. Retain vocal membership,outside guitar capability table;218W×143Dmm also exceeds preference. Dedicated PHONES/LINE remains separate. No hardware test ordownload.

[VE500-S] |

PSA-S **500 mA is adapter capacity**, distinct from the VE-500’s **310 mA with phantom /210 mA without** draw. Actual seller-supplied adapter, regional plug and input label still need confirmation. |
| USB audio / confirmed functions | **USB audio and MIDI supported**, explicitly stated in owner manual p3. Parameter Guide p30 provides **IN LEVEL and OUT LEVEL 0–200**, computer audio routing **MIX** (internal mixer) or **PRE FX** (Input EQ then effects), and **LOOP BACK** to return computer audio mixed with VE-500 audio to the computer. LOOP BACK ON disables ROUTING selection. Earlier “USB audio not established” observations above are superseded by this2026-10-18 manual check; channel count and audio format remain unverified. [VE500-M];[VE500-P] |
| USB audio / monitoring persistence | DIRECT MON controls the direct VE-500 signal at the outputs. It **cannot be saved and resets ON at power-up**. OFF leaves USB-return audio at the outputs and requires computer audio-through for monitoring the processed input. Avoid treating OFF as a persistent stand-alone mute setting. [VE500-P p30] |
| Power / DC switching evidence | Owner manual p8 says inserting the adapter into DC IN powers the unit on and disconnecting it powers it off. Its supply table lists AC adapter; no explicit battery or USB-bus-power capability was located. Those capabilities remain unverified rather than inferred from the USB connector or audio support. [VE500-M pp8,19] |
| Editor / MIDI | Windows/Mac full editor/librarian;USB-MIDI and DIN MIDI can guide harmony/pitch. [VE500-S]USB driver required by owner manual;patch edit/name/reorder/backup distinct from firmware. [VE500-M] Initial2026-10-16 OS gap;see separately dated2026-10-17 compatibility rows below. |
| Synth / acoustic / loading | Guitar input can excite vocoder/talkbox sound;THRU remains unprocessed. [VE500-S] This does not establish guitar amp/acoustic multifx,IR,native NAM,converted NAM or capture loading. |
| Category choices / Enhance and pitch correction | ENHANCE combines definition, compression, de-essing and EQ controls; these are not alternative block types. PITCH CORRECT has **four types: SOFT, HARD, ELECTRIC, ROBOT**, and CHROMATIC/KEY scale settings. [VE500-P p5] |
| Category choices / harmony parts | **Three harmony parts**, each with voice, pan, level and character controls. Four user-interval maps are settings, not four extra simultaneous voices. Selecting USR-INT disables MIDI/INST chord detection. Double has LIGHT/NORMAL/DEEP intensity plus OFF. [VE500-P pp6–7] |
| Category choices / vocoder | **Three types: STANDARD, VINTAGE, TALK BOX**. Sources: INST, MIDI, MIDI(USB), MIC, AUTO. INST carrier has six choices (SAW, VINTAGE SAW, DETUNE SAW, SQUARE, RECTANGLE, DISTORTION); non-INST replaces DISTORTION with SUPER SAW. Source/carrier choices are parameters, not additional simultaneous engines. HARMONY and VOCODER remain alternatives. [VE500-P pp6–7] |
| Categories / FX1–4 inventory | **20 alternatives per engine**:Distortion,Radio,Lo-Fi,Filter,T.Wah,Ring Modulator,Chorus,Flanger,Tremolo,Phaser,Rotary,Slicer,Isolator,Vibrato,Pan,Roll,Freeze,Granular Delay,Delay,Reverb. Same type can occupy multiple engines. These are choices,not80 simultaneous effects. [VE500-P p8] |
| Categories / reverb engine distinction | FX Reverb has **5 types**:Ambience,Room,Hall,Plate,Mod;0.1–10s,pre-delay0–100ms. Each dedicated REV1/REV2 has those5 **plusDelay=6 choices**. Its reverb pre-delay1 is0–200ms,pre-delay2Off/0–500ms;Delay1–2000ms/BPM. Do not copy dedicated-REV ranges intoFX Reverb. [VE500-P pp13–14] |
| Looper / location | **8 positions**:MICIN,PRE FX1/2/3/4,PRE REV1/2,OUTPUT. Position changes where loop audio is captured relative to processing;not8 independent loopers. [VE500-P p17] |
| Editor / Windows qualification | Editor **1.00** page listsWindows10,8.1,7SP1+;requires productdriver. Separate driver **1.0.1 (Dec2021)** supportsWindows11/10x64;Win10x86 remains1.0.0. Driver excludesARM PCs,virtualWindows,Windows onMac and10Mobile. Driver11support alone does not certify editor11support. [VE500-EW];[VE500-DW] |
| Editor / current macOS matrix | Checked2026-10-17,matrixupdatedSep16 2026:VE500 **Hardware&Driver** and **ExclusiveApplication** bothYes(Download) for15/26;27UnderTesting. Editor1.00 requires driver;Mac driver1.0.3 is listed separately. This is dedicated-software support,not class-compliant/no-driver proof. [VE500-C];[VE500-EM];[VE500-DM] |
| Firmware / live qualifications | OwnSystemProgram1.10(Jan2025)changesAutoOff optionsOff/20min/10h,default20min. 1.07(Dec2021)fixesLEADFX withLOOP MICIN and permits knob patchselection beyondPATCH EXTENT;1.05(Jan2019)fixes some editor parameter settings;1.04(Nov2018)fixes externalMIDI withoutactive-sensing problems. Seller installedversion unknown;not claims thatcurrentfirmware retains oldbugs. [VE500-F] |

### VE-500 — MIDI visibility, reset scope and guitar detection

Checked 2026-10-18 in Roland’s own support articles.

| Field | Evidence / qualification |
|---|---|
| MIDI / harmony menu visibility | **M>VOICE**, HRM1–3 page5, appears only when a MIDI device is connected through the **5-pin MIDI IN** connector. Missing page5 is therefore not proof that the parameter is absent. This specific condition does not establish equivalent USB-only visibility. [Roland article, updated2026-05-04](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/360016423292-VE-500-MIDI-to-Voice-Parameters-Not-Visible-in-HRM-Effect) |
| Presets / selective reset | Factory Reset FROM/TO can select **SYSTEM or U01–U99**. SYSTEM-only or a selected user-patch range can be reset; affected settings are lost. This is not evidence of additional memories or a stored-loop backup. [Roland article, updated2026-07-02](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/360015997611-VE-500-How-do-I-restore-the-factory-settings-Factory-Reset) |
| Routing / chord detection | Roland recommends guitar → VE-500 INST, then unprocessed THRU → external guitar processor. Sending an already effected guitar into VE-500 is discouraged because dry guitar gives better chord recognition. This does not add guitar processing to the vocal unit. [Roland connection guidance](https://support.roland.com/hc/en-us/articles/17572594030619-How-to-connect-a-BOSS-GT-001-to-a-VE-500-or-VE-8) |

Checked2026-10-28 against [own Parameter Guide][VE500-P],MENU→PLAY→EXP HLD,manufacturer-authored indexed text. **ON carries expression state across patch changes only when FUNCTION matches**;the manual’s example uses PAT LEVEL in both patches. OFF does not carry that state. This is conditional position recall,not a global volume-target assignment. Global assignment andvolume endpoints/placement/output coverage remain unverified here.

No dedicated headphone socket in the published inventory. No hardware test ordownload.

### VF-1 — preset assignments, recall and effects-off scope

[Manufacturer-authored owner manual reproduction,p33](https://www.manualslib.com/manual/315068/Boss-Vf-1.html?page=33) explicitly makes Control Assign independent per patch,with four assignment slots andtargetMIN/MAX. Targets include Master Level andFoot Volume/Foot Level. **A target called Master is not proof of an all-preset assignment override.** Original model/footer andpage content identified;the host144-page body differs fromthe official146-page file containing supplemental material. No complete byte-equivalence orvisual inspection claimed.

[Indexed official SYSTEM SETTING,p43](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/VF-1_OM.pdf) documents Assign Hold:On carries controller operation into the next patch;Off uses its saved target value. This is recall scope,not assignment propagation. Effects Off mode choosesDIRECT input passthrough orMUTE for all outputs;expression attenuation ofDIRECT,phones,tails andloops remains unverified. Display contrast1–16 is not aphysical display-size specification.

### VF-1 — original algorithm census and creative note handling

Checked **2026-11-04** in the manufacturer-authored [original manual reproduction](https://manualzz.com/doc/6998079/boss-vf-1-multiple-effects-processor-owner-manual), TOC andprintedpp47–48,92; [official indexed Guitar Synth,p99](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/VF-1_OM.pdf);and original [p100](https://www.manualslib.com/manual/315068/Boss-Vf-1.html?page=100) / [p101](https://www.manualslib.com/manual/315068/Boss-Vf-1.html?page=101). Original document headings/footer match the prior audit;OCR is imperfect,so no diagram/visual census orfirmware equivalence is claimed. Host-generated explanations excluded.

| Capability | Verified distinction and limit |
|---|---|
| Algorithm architecture | Original headline **46 effects /36 algorithms**;TOC independently enumerates PB1–PB36,with names retained in the ledger. Algorithms are effect combinations,not36 concurrent slots. Speaker Modeling is the separately documented addendum,not silently included in the original36. MOD/SFX choices depend on the selected algorithm. |
| Guitar Synth | In Guitar Multi1’s MOD slot. **Single notes required**:SQR/SAW use detected pitch/attack to drive an internal source;BRASS/BOW reshape input audio. Chromatic andoctave controls apply toSQR/SAW;PWM onlySQR. Hold also applies onlySQR/SAW. Brass/Bow release follows input decay despite longer Release settings. |
| Harmonist | Guitar Multi1 MOD→HR. **FAST/MEDIUM/SLOW accept chords** with response/modulation trade-off;**MONO andHARMONY require single notes**. One/two generated voices andmono/stereo output choices do not change these input-note rules. Up/down two-octave range documented;five user scales are separate data. |
| Instrument / special processing | **Acoustic GtSIM** explicitly converts electric-guitar tone toward acoustic;Bass GTR SIM toward bass. SFX inGuitar Multi2 selects acoustic/bass simulation,Slow Gear,Feedbacker,Pickup Sim orTremolo/Pan—alternatives,not six concurrent blocks. ACOUSTIC processing andMic Simulator are distinct roles. |

MOD andSFX positions are algorithm-dependent;the guide prohibits placing MOD,SFX orPreamp/Speaker afterMASTER. Its FV marker locates the assigned Foot Volume within each algorithm,not a universal output stage. These findings supersede the baseline’s synth/acoustic unknowns only within this documented scope. Otherpitch algorithms,exactsimultaneous ceilings,tracking/latency,currentfirmware andmarket gaps remain unverified. No download ordevice operation.

| Control / scope | Documented behavior |
|---|---|
| EXP1/EXP2 function | MEMORY LEVEL100 controls patch level0–100;MEMORY LEVEL200 controls0–200. Combine with SYSTEM preference for global volume assignment. These are parameter units,not dB. |

### VG-800 — bypass and computer-audio routing boundaries

[Reference eng01,©2025,pp5–6,11–14](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/VG-800_reference_eng01_W.pdf): approved analog expression models are EV-30,FV-500L,FV-500H andEV-5,using sharedEXP sockets. This doesnot certify arbitrary controllers orpotentiometer wiring. Effects/output/send-return blocks can be reordered orarranged inparallel;the illustrated FV positions arenot universal. **FX BYPASS** isolates INST modeling,whereas tuner BYPASS passes input unchanged andTHRU retains the current memory. These are distinct operating modes;Memory Level coverage ineach remains unverified.

[Parameter Guide eng01,©2025,pp48–49,51](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/VG-800_parameter_eng01_W.pdf):

| Route / mode | Exact scope |
|---|---|
| USB STANDARD | Computer playback mixes toOUTPUT;USB recording contains VG-800 audio only. |
| USB MIX | Mixed computer+processor audio goes toOUTPUT andUSB. |
| RE-GUITAR/RE-BASS | Reprocesses recorded divided-pickup audio;requiresVENDOR driver mode. |
| Driver / levels | VENDOR uses dedicated driver;GENERIC uses OS-standard driver. Driver mode cannot change whileUSB connected. USB MIX LEVEL andOUT LEVEL independently range0–200. |
| Serial GK Audio | OFF disables;MIX combines incoming stereo audio withunit output forGK OUT;OUT sends unit stereo output;THRU forwards incoming audio. Separate MIX/OUT levels0–200. This isnot the divided-string pickup stream. |

Parameter p51 specifically describes tuner BYPASS as unchanged **divided-pickup** input;Reference p11 uses broader guitar-input wording. Normal-input coverage isnot resolved bythat difference. No demonstrated pedal mute ofcomputer playback,USB sends,GK Audio orheadphones follows fromthese routing descriptions. Tails/looper coverage andcurrent-firmware equivalence remain open;no hardware test ordownload performed.

[Tone Studio1.1.0 Windows](https://www.boss.info/global/support/by_product/vg-800/updates_drivers/0da39458-ba02-4835-94c0-fc2bd950b644/) and[Mac](https://www.boss.info/global/support/by_product/vg-800/updates_drivers/fee13637-22c1-49cb-8438-1e68e814d6b5/),July2026,**require firmware1.50 orlater andthe product driver**. They reduce memory-switch device-lock time andfix ASSIGN/GK SETTING display issues. Mac page explicitly supports Apple silicon andIntel;exact OS compatibility isdelegated tothe current matrix. ### VG-800 — synth polyphony versus single-note FX

[Parameter Guide eng01,©2025,pp4,14–18,22–27](https://static.roland.com/assets/media/pdf/VG-800_parameter_eng01_W.pdf),checked2026-11-05;installed/current-firmware equivalence remains unverified:

| Capability | Exact distinction |
|---|---|
| SYNTH | GR-300:guitar mode;ANALOG GR:bass mode;both explicitly polyphonic. Other names:SOLO,FILTER BASS,CRYSTAL,ORGAN;their input-note polyphony isunverified. Retain Serial GK modeling requirement. |
| Harmony | INST alternate-tuning HARMONY analyzes each string. FX HARMONIST instead requires single notes andmuting other strings;these are different processors. |
| Pitch FX | PITCH SHIFTER±24semitones:MONO requires single notes;FAST/MEDIUM/SLOW chord support unverified. PEDAL BEND±24also requires single notes. OCTAVE/OCTAVE BASS addsone/two octaves down;chord handling unverified. |
| Instrument processing | DEFRETTER/DEFRETTER BASS andSITAR SIM;AC RESONANCE processes acoustic-pickup sound,not proof ofelectric-to-acoustic conversion. INST acoustic models remain separate. |
| Special | HUMANIZER vowel processing,LO-FI,SLICER andSLOW GEAR. SOUND HOLD requires single notes;its trigger iswritten OFF,not saved loop audio. FX1–FX3 arethree selectable slots,not simultaneous access toevery named effect. |

No measured tracking/latency claim orownership inference;the partial record remains outside402.

These names/version labels are reproduced fromthe manufacturer matrix. “Under Testing” isnot certified compatibility or“No”. Download status requires dedicated software;it doesnot certify all future OS releases orindividual machines. Existing Windows ARM/VM/Mac exclusions andTone Studio1.1.0/firmware1.50 requirements stillapply.

[Roland’s Windows10 support notice](https://www.roland.com/global/support/support_news/2509161000/) discontinues related drivers/updates,operation guarantees andusage/troubleshooting support from **2025-10-14**. Downloads mayremain available;availability isnot ongoing support. This qualifies the older Windows10/11 driver headline rather than erasing it. Mac driver detail page stilltimes out;the matrix resolves the listed OS statuses withoutestablishing its full driver-specific restrictions.
